in writing. - when the european parliament voted today on a resolution on the commission communication on strengthening the social dimension of economic and monetary union, i chose to vote in favour. given the high unemployment rates in many eu member states, i welcome the commission's initiative to strengthen the social dimension. the free movement of workers is at the heart of this, and it must be put into practice. in this context, it is important to point out that the main responsibility for labour market and social policy lies with the individual national governments. in the resolution i therefore voted against the text on the establishment of a social pillar in economic and monetary union, as well as against the far-reaching amendments such as the establishment of a european unemployment compensation scheme.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i and many of my colleagues thought that slavery and forced labor would be abolished by now. but the model of the visa system applied in qatar shows that it continues, and worse still, we are actively contributing to it through fifa's decision to put the world cup in qatar. it is also clear that there has been massive money transfers, sometimes called bribes, and that the way the arenas are to be built exploits the system of forced labour. we cannot accept that. if qatar does not immediately promise to abolish these systems and to comply with the ilo's demands, we should demand that fifa actually change its seat. if you cannot guarantee basic human rights, you cannot organise a world cup.
for the verts/ale group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. from next year, the eu will have a completely new fisheries policy. it will mean an end to overfishing, an end to the dumping of fish and much stricter conditions for eu fleets when fishing outside europe's borders in developing countries. this is a slightly revolutionary change from a much-discussed destructive fisheries policy that has given the eu a bad name around the world, and i am very proud to say that it is the european parliament, the environmental movement and also us in the green group who have been driving this reform. it shows that change is possible in other areas too, if politicians take their job seriously and voters clearly show what they expect from their elected representatives. but the fight is not over. now we must translate the good law into action. any new decision in the fisheries sector must respect the new sustainability principles. that is why i think that tomorrow parliament should vote against the fisheries agreement with morocco, which does not contain the clear clauses on respect for human rights that we have agreed. i would like to thank all the colleagues who have worked, in particular mrs rodust and commissioner damanaki, for a very good job.
mr president, i would like to thank you. in the more than 20 years that i have been out speaking to the public on european issues, fisheries policy has been one of the most criticised areas of all. it's coming up in schools, it's coming up in workplaces, it's coming up in town meetings. but now we are finally moving towards the completion of a fundamental reform of the eu's fisheries policy, and for that i think we owe a special thanks to commissioner damanaki and our main rapporteur, mrs ulrike rodust. we have some decisions to make, including on deep sea fishing, where i hope we can stick to the basic principle of sustainability and establish a ban on the most harmful deep sea radiation. if we can put the final pieces of the puzzle together, i think we can proudly say that we have shaped a much more sustainable fisheries policy for the future, environmentally and economically. in the long term, this will also benefit the fishing industry.
madam president, i would like to thank you. i have to tell commissioner maria that she is my hero. as for the need to introduce a new fisheries reform, i have to say that this time i do not agree. i do not think that is true. this deal is not in accordance with international law. the resources in the form of fish that are off the coast of western sahara belong to the people of western sahara, and whether or not the condition is met, you have to ask them, and they have not been asked. i'm saying that we're on an incredibly dangerous path. if we review the assessment made by the united nations - the united nations which in a resolution said that western sahara is occupied and its legal service which on an earlier occasion said that the agreement is not compatible - how can we dare to criticize russia or the united states or israel or anyone else the next time they say that the un has made a wrong assessment? i say we say no to this.
madam president, tomorrow – 10 december – is actually human rights day, which was proclaimed by the un general assembly in 1950. this is quite ironical. i would like to remind colleagues that last week the pan-african parliament and the african union urged this parliament to vote ‘no’ to this agreement because it violates human rights and the self-determination of western sahara. the 18 difficult months that the commissioner was talking about: why were they so difficult? because morocco did not want to have a human rights clause in this protocol. so the wording of the human rights clause in this protocol differs from any other fisheries agreement that we have ever seen in the committee on fisheries. they did not even want to mention ‘western sahara’; the words ‘western saharaʼ are not there. so how are we going to check how this protocol is going to be executed when morocco does not even allow members of this parliament to come into the country to meet with sahrawi people and meet the human rights activists in the area? i very much urge colleagues to stand up for human rights tomorrow on human rights day and vote ‘no’ to this shameful agreement.
for the alde group. president. - the debate is closed. we are still forced to acknowledge that the roma minority lives in a harsher and more difficult reality than all of us other eu citizens. they are europe's most vulnerable minority today, and the abuse never seems to stop. recently, this was also evident in my home country of sweden when it was revealed that the police had extensive registration of roma. the registers contained unpunished people, including deceased persons and children under two years of age. the only thing that's indecent is the first name! the national strategies adopted are not producing sufficient results and the money allocated has not reached the roma as it should. i therefore call on the commission to set up a monitoring mechanism in all member states to ensure the fundamental rights of roma. today, the eurobarometer report on the priorities of citizens was released. the survey was carried out in all the member states and the priority given to the eu and its institutions as the primary task is the safeguarding of human rights. then there is solidarity and equal rights. now is the time for all the eu institutions to deliver what the citizens are prioritizing and asking for. in today's eu, everyone should have equal opportunities for schooling, economic and social rights, free movement and access to healthcare. now we must intensify cooperation and show political will to tackle these problems. and it is now!
for the alde group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. in our daily work here in parliament, we are busy negotiating and adopting regulations and directives, but we sometimes forget that these are the ones that are then to be interpreted by our lawyers, judges and prosecutors and other legal experts across the eu. however, as regards legal traditions, there are great differences between our member states, and for the rules to work well it is important that those who interpret these rules are given good opportunities to get into each other's legal systems. further training of judges, prosecutors and other legal experts is therefore important and the value of these face-to-face meetings, which can lead to increased trust between those participating in these training sessions, cannot be underestimated. the work we are doing in the judicial field is about mutual recognition of legal acts and decisions, but to move forward properly we also need to increase our confidence, not only in the common european systems but also in each other's national systems. i have occasionally had the privilege of speaking at events organised by the academy of european law and of witnessing first-hand the interesting discussions that arise between experts from different countries exchanging ideas and thoughts, and last week i had the opportunity to meet young judges from across the eu who were taking part in a pilot project. you could really see the trust growing between them. i'm very happy about that. the equality, rights and citizenship programme for the period 2014-2020 exists to promote the rights of individuals and to protect the fundamental rights and freedoms of all citizens. we must never forget that the eu was created to protect the rights of individuals, not states. in the work of this new programme, it has been very important to ensure that the long-term work on violence against women and children continues to be given proper space. the programme shall continue to promote all the essential values of eu citizens and also support programmes on non-discrimination, gender equality for disabled people, data protection and the rights of both children and older people. the focus is on promoting transnational cooperation and knowledge-raising activities. i welcome these two renewed programmes for the coming years. they will lead to a more stable value base and to increased trust and respect for our differences.
in writing. - there are many beautiful phrases about fundamental civil rights in the programme. but many of the phrases in the proposed programme are pure hypocrisy in view of the policy pursued by the european commission towards the countries in the euro crisis, where people are losing the most basic rights because of austerity policies. the report also calls for the establishment of a european union-wide network of experts in the field of gender equality and women's rights.
in writing. − the eu-ivory coast fisheries agreement is a standard agreement which is a good step towards a more sustainable fisheries policy. but the eu should basically encourage the development of its own fishing industry, through aid and other means. on this basis, i chose to abstain from voting.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report to provide the opportunity for retraining and a second chance on the labour market for the workers concerned. but to really create jobs in europe, we need a new economic policy that involves green investment and public sector investment. we need an end to austerity, we need regulation of the financial markets and an organised winding-up of the euro.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to cover the use of the european globalisation adjustment fund. since 2009, i have voted against all proposals to use the fund as a rule. this is because i do not think that the fund is the right instrument to help workers affected by structural change. the fund has now grown, evolved and received its own allocation. it will therefore remain for the foreseeable future. to benefit from the fund, applicants must meet very strict requirements for co-financing and long-term employment measures. all three cases at issue meet these criteria. this includes support for continuing training and support for new training, all with the aim of reintegrating workers into the labour market. europe and the eu can respond to competition and structural changes in the market in much better, more efficient and more sustainable ways in the long term. but now the fund is there, and it's about making the best of the situation. in view of this, i have therefore voted in favour of the three reports in question.

in writing. - i am not opposed to a european disaster management mechanism, where the eu can coordinate and complement the member states' actions in the field of civil protection. but since i have serious doubts about the proposed military component, i choose to abstain.
in writing. - we believe it is important that decisions on issues relating to abortion and sexual rights are taken at member state level. in the european union there are both those who share the values and views of sweden and those who have values that lead to opposite views. we are happy to express our views on free abortion and sexual rights, but we do not believe that parliament should act as a parliament above the member states. in view of this, we cannot vote against the member states deciding on the matters at issue. it is our firm opinion that it should remain so. subsidiarity must be applied here. we moderates intended to support a liberal and modern view on abortion and sexual rights in today's vote, while emphasising subsidiarity and the fact that these issues are the competence of the member states. we were going to vote for the estrelara report. we did not, however, want to vote against a resolution which emphasized that these are subsidiarity issues. we therefore refrained from giving our views on the substance of the matter, contrary to the epp group and its resolution on subsidiarity. when the resolution on subsidiarity won, the issues were never decided.
in writing. - when the european parliament voted today on the report on sexual and reproductive health and rights, i chose to support amendment no 2. the proposal stresses that the eu should respect the responsibility of the member states to decide on their health care policy. however, let me say that i firmly believe that legislation should give women the right to make the crucial decision on abortion in the first trimester of pregnancy. at the same time, it is important to emphasise that the state and the legislator cannot remain neutral in the face of the ethical dilemma that abortion undeniably entails. the report does not mention at all that the protective value of the foetus must also be taken into account at some point during pregnancy. this is surprising, and it reduces the complexity of these ethically difficult issues. every country, inside and outside the eu, has every reason to improve and strengthen gender equality and respect for the life and health of every individual. however, i believe that the issues raised in the report do not fall within the competence of the european parliament under the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. - we swedish social democrats believe it is urgent to support voluntary work, especially in the field of associations. a vibrant civil society is an essential component of democracy. community life gives people a chance to meet, socialize and exchange experiences. a vibrant associative life also promotes the opportunities for people to make political demands and develop democracy. the social democrats believe that non-profit involvement is very valuable. that is why we support the report. however, it is important that voluntary work does not replace paid work. if voluntary work is to be carried out in the private or public sector, it must be done in dialogue with the social partners and with respect for any collective agreements.
in writing. - we voted today in favour of the report on voluntary service in europe. we are, however, opposed to the creation of a new fund.

the committee on development and cooperation has been asked to submit an opinion. - mr president! - what is it? i would just like to mention two points from the deve opinion. the first concerns migration policy. human rights are universal and apply to all people, including refugees. it is therefore important that the eu lives as we teach. tragedies such as that in lampedusa undermine the eu's credibility in the field of human rights when acting in international contexts. secondly, something about women's rights. human rights are universal and apply to all people, including women. despite this, the rights of women and girls are violated every day. patriarchy is like a persistent epidemic that denies women the right to a life of dignity worldwide. the eu must therefore work to ensure that the new millennium goals are clearly gender-sensitive, that they strengthen women's rights and emphasise women's sexual and reproductive rights. i therefore agree with much of what the council representative said here today.
mr president, i would like to thank you. we have spoken earlier today about ukraine and the situation there. if you turn on the tv, it's all about the central african republic. two different countries, to be sure, but one thing applies to the citizens of both these countries and of all other countries: that man is of inviolable value and that human rights and freedoms apply to everyone, wherever he lives. and as was said here earlier, these values are universal. there are no values and no dignity that a president can give away, nor take back, but it is about the man because he is a man. i think it is extremely important that the eu should insist on the fact that these are universal values and not start to relativise these values on the basis of language, culture, religion, tradition and whatever else we may have to count. it is satisfactory, mr president, that we now have a special representative for human rights and freedoms. i would like to see every government have a representative of this kind.
the s&d group is in favour of the proposal. president. - the debate is closed. let me begin by emphasizing that it is positive that the fight against tax fraud, tax evasion and aggressive tax planning is now being stepped up at a rapid pace. this work is absolutely necessary to create a level playing field and to strengthen public finances. a significant reduction in tax fraud is a crucial key to sustainably kick-starting growth and job creation in the european economy. in my view, the commission's proposal to extend the automatic exchange of information is very good. it is in line with international developments in the field, not least the fatca process. it imposes very reasonable transparency requirements on the member states. it shall streamline the exchange of data by using a uniform european model. the only changes needed are minor additions on integrity, sanctions, resources and the commission's continued role in this area. such documents were also voted on in committee. in this context, it is very surprising that a large number of members in this parliament are actually trying to water down the proposal. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of personal data. in my view, such a weakening would be very unfortunate. the result is a less efficient system that would partly contravene the fatca model. that's not what europe needs. the only people who would benefit from this are the tax evaders. i therefore call on those members who advocate these weakening measures to reconsider their position. if parliament is to maintain its credibility in the fight against tax evasion, the line cannot be to undermine the proposals put forward.
for the alde group. president. - the debate is closed. the financial crisis has sparked outrage over lost and withheld tax revenues. taxation is no longer a national issue. in times of crisis and austerity, solidarity is required from all. we all have to pay our fair share. no one should get away with clever cross-border solutions. it is important to increase tax revenues, clear welfare, assist entrepreneurship, support jobs and growth. i therefore believe that it is right that we need a strong eu framework to strengthen the fight against tax fraud and tax evasion. i therefore welcome this directive and the extended directive. it is positive that the automatic exchange of information is being changed to extend income and capital to assets and capital gains on financial accounts. it is also important to implement, as a next step, the automatic exchange of information between the eu and third countries, particularly the united states. finally, the liberal group, alde, has tabled amendments, in which we refer to an opinion of the european data protection supervisor. unfortunately, the edps delivered his opinion on the same day as our vote in the committee on budgets, which is why this could not be taken into account in the report. in our amendments 27 to 31 we have therefore put forward proposals to strengthen the right of individuals to the protection of their personal data. this is in line with the edps's opinion. mr commissioner, you raised this issue in your comments, but i do think we need more protection for individuals in this text and therefore i hope that you can support it. you said that it doesn't belong here, it is not needed, but in these days when this protection is under fire and called into question i do hope that you can support these amendments.
mr president, first of all regarding growth and jobs: i think it is important to underline the fact that those member states who have applied a policy of spending have got themselves deeper into the crisis while those who have used a policy of reforms are either out of the crisis or slowly coming out of it. i think it is important for the european council to remember that. second, when we discuss the openness of the european union and the freedom to move and the freedom to work everywhere: we should not see that as a threat. that is the strength of the european union; a political strength, but also some of our fundamental values that we need to safeguard. third, discussing the most fundamental important values of democracy, stability and peace in europe, i think it is worthwhile to underline that just now, in one of the european capitals, hundreds of thousands of people are demonstrating for the european union. it is important to remember and to respect, because they see the promises of stability, rule of law, freedom, democracy and transparent societies: they are demonstrating for that. it is a formative time and we need to respond to that. they are not demonstrating against anything other than those who are trying to impose other forces, other hidden powers. parliamentary decisions had already been reached that ukraine should enter a closer agreement with the european union, but we all know that other decisions have been taken in the dark. we need to support those people and that is also the most important way of safeguarding european peace and security. if we cannot respond to that, we need to further discuss how to best develop the european union.
in writing. − we have chosen not to support the eu globalisation fund for the years 2014-2020. we believe that the existing eu instruments should be used primarily to increase the employability of those who have been notified or dismissed. it should be made clear that social policy is primarily the responsibility of the member states and that trade is good. however, europe is still in crisis and in times of crisis special measures are needed. therefore, the allocation of the egf may be eligible for a certain period in the future.
in writing. the european parliament's report on human rights in the world is important in promoting and consolidating democracy in the wider world. i voted for the report but i think it is very regrettable that the situation in eritrea is not mentioned. eritrea is a military dictatorship under president afewerki and one of the most closed countries in the world. every month, nearly 4,000 people flee the horrific situation in the country. freedom of expression is restricted and among the tens of thousands of political prisoners in the country there are 28 journalists, one of whom is eu citizen dawit isaak. the european parliament should draw attention to the state of the dictatorship in eritrea to show that we will not tolerate these violations of the fundamental rights of the people.
in writing. − the retail sector provides one in seven jobs in europe – that is nearly 30 million jobs and a true pillar of our economy. i have long fought to put the real economy at the centre of our agenda. therefore i am glad that, following my report on a more efficient and fairer retail market in europe in 2011, we are continuing to keep the retail sector high on our agenda. with the retail action plan we have a clear set of priorities to achieve a single market for retail. now it is time for national and local authorities to step up their efforts to ensure free movement and establishment across borders, facilitate equal access for smes, remove discriminatory practices and promote e-commerce. i would particularly like to commend the voluntary supply chain initiative by stakeholders to deal with unfair practices in b2b relations. i encourage all parties to pursue the constructive dialogue to bring farmersʼ associations too on board and make sure that the enforcement mechanisms provide concrete results fast.
the s&d group is in favour of the proposal. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. the european payment account market consisting of banks and other payment service providers has several shortcomings. the pricing is messy and it's often difficult to change banks. the most serious problem is that today there are 25 million europeans who want an account but do not have one. this creates an exclusion. it's very difficult to function in a modern society without access to an account. i am satisfied with the agreement reached by the committee on budgets. with such a framework, the shortcomings should be able to be remedied. first, the price information for payment accounts will be clearer. secondly, it will make it easier to change banks both nationally and across borders. thirdly, the right of all consumers to open and use a basic bank account should be established. banks should only be able to refuse if they suspect criminal activity. the costs of the account shall be limited to what is reasonable. overpricing which in practice excludes consumers shall not be permitted. however, there are two points on which i am not entirely satisfied with the agreement reached by the committee. the possibility for member states to exempt banks from the requirement to offer basic accounts is too great. the requirement for consumers to provide a 'genuine link' to the country where they wish to open an account may create difficulties. these two items risk making the basic account inaccessible. it's also the wrong way to go. instead, the basic account must be made as easily accessible as possible. because it requires that virtually all banks offer the basic account and that all unnecessary red tape is avoided. i hope that the problems on these points will be reduced in the vote tomorrow and that fully functional solutions can then be found in the trilogue with the council. the basic account must not be a matter of stigmatisation and charity. the account must be a real right that can be exercised without any hassle. i would also like to thank the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs for a very good job.
